Construction of pre- and post-Lie algebras for stochastic PDEs
Loïc Foissy

Abstract
We give and study a construction of pre-Lie algebra structures on rooted trees whose edges and vertices are decorated, with a grafting product acting, through a map ϕ \phi , both on the decoration of the created edge and on the vertex that holds the grafting. We show that this construction gives a pre-Lie algebra if, and only if, the map ϕ \phi satisfies a commutation relation, called tree-compatibility. We show how to extend this pre-Lie algebra structure to a post-Lie one by a semi-direct extension with another post-Lie algebra. We also define several constructions to obtain tree-compatible maps, and give examples, including a description of all tree-admissible maps when the space of decorations of the vertices is 2 2 -dimensional and the space of decorations of the edges is finite-dimensional. A particular example of such a construction is used by Bruned, Hairer and Zambotti for the study of stochastic partial differential equations: when no noise is involved, we show that the underlying tree-compatible map is the exponential of a simpler one and deduce an explicit isomorphism with a classical pre-Lie algebra of rooted trees; when a noise is involved, we obtain the underlying tree-compatible map as a direct sum. We also obtain with our formalism the post-Lie algebras described by Bruned and Katsetsiadis.



Keywords.
pre-Lie algebra; post-Lie algebra; tree-compatibility

AMS classification.
17A30 17D25 05C05

Introduction

Pre-Lie algebras, also known under the name Gerstenhaber or left-symmetric algebras, since their introduction by Vinberg
[
34
]
and Gerstenhaber
[
20
]
in the sixties,
are now widely used in numerous area of mathematics. A pre-Lie algebra is a vector space with a bilinear product
\triangleleft
satisfying the axiom

x\triangleleft(y\triangleleft z)-(x\triangleleft y)\triangleleft z=y\triangleleft(x\triangleleft z)-(y\triangleleft x)\triangleleft z.

Consequently, the antisymmetrization of
\triangleleft
is a Lie bracket.
A typical example is given by the flat and torsion free connection on a locally Euclidean space, see
[
17
]
for more details on these geometric aspects.
Chapoton and Livernet
[
9
]
gave a construction of the operad of pre-Lie algebras, combinatorially described by rooted trees
with an operadic composition based on insertion of a tree at a vertex of another tree. Equivalently, the free pre-Lie algebra generated by a set
D_{V}
is the vector space
of rooted trees whose vertices are decorated by elements of
D_{V}
, with the pre-Lie product defined by graftings. Here is an example of computation in a free pre-Lie algebra:
if
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5}\in D_{V}
,

It is noticeable that this formalism of decorated trees, and consequently a more or less hidden pre-Lie structure, appears in different works and domains:
numerical analysis, Runge-Kutta methods and Butcher’s series
[
4
,
21
,
23
]
,
Quantum Field Theory and Renormalization in the work of Connes and Kreimer
[
10
]
,
Ecalle’s mould calculus and arborification’s process
[
12
,
15
,
16
]
, etc.

In the last decade, Bruned, Hairer and Zambotti
[
5
,
8
,
24
]
introduced and used Regularity Structures, in order to study a wide class of stochastic partial differential equations (shortly, sPDEs).
They are based on Hopf algebraic structures built on rooted trees, with numerous decorations on vertices and edges, which interact in some sense. Dually, we obtain a structure on these trees,
with a family of grafting products (one for each possible decoration of an edge), which modify the decoration of the vertex on which it is grafted. For example, in a one-dimensional case,
where vertices and edges are decorated by natural integers
a,a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5}\in\mathbb{N}
,

In fact, this can be even more intricate when one consider noises, which needs other decorations
\Xi
for the edges and
\star
(represented by an absence of decoration in
[
7
]
) for the vertices. For example,

In this formalism, edges may be decorated by pairs of elements in
\mathcal{T}\times\mathbb{N}^{d+1}
, where
\mathcal{T}
is a set, whose decorations are "inert" and do not interact.
This structure is a multiple pre-Lie algebra, also called matching pre-Lie algebra, see
[
18
,
35
]
.
These are objects with a family
\triangleleft=(\triangleleft_{a})_{a\in D_{E}}
of products indexed by the set of decorations
D_{E}
, with the following compatibility: for any
a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E}
,

x\triangleleft_{a}(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z)-(x\triangleleft_{a}y)\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z=y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}(x\triangleleft_{a}z)-(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}x)\triangleleft_{a}z.

When
D_{E}
is a singleton, we recover (classical) pre-Lie algebras. Free multiple pre-Lie algebras have been described in
[
7
,
18
]
in terms of rooted trees whose edges are decorated by elements of
D_{E}
and vertices by elements of a set
D_{V}
,
corresponding to generators. Here is an example of computation in a free multiple pre-Lie algebra: if
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5}\in D_{V}
and
a,a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in D_{E}
,

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 31.38889pt\raise-62.4444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.55554pt\raise-62.4444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

In fact, the multiple pre-lie algebra structure
\triangleleft^{\mathrm{s}}
used for sPDEs without noise, is shown in
[
7
, Theorem 2.7]
to be isomorphic to the free one
\triangleleft
, by a triangularity argument.

Another way to consider multiple pre-Lie algebras is to see them as
D_{E}
-graded homogeneous pre-Lie algebras: in a clearer way, if
A
is a multiple pre-Lie algebra, then
\mathbb{K}D_{E}\otimes V
is a pre-Lie algebra, with the product defined by

\displaystyle\forall a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E},\>\forall x,x^{\prime}\in A,

\displaystyle a\otimes x\triangleleft a^{\prime}\otimes x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}\otimes(x\triangleleft_{a}x^{\prime}).

When the set
D_{E}
have more structures, variations are possible, such as family pre-Lie algebras
[
27
]
or more general objects
[
19
]
. Here is an example of computation in a free family pre-Lie algebra.
Here, the set
D_{E}
of decorations of the edges is a commutative semi-group, whose product is denoted by
*
.

In all these examples, the decorations of the edges can be modified, but the decorations of the vertices are left untouched, a major difference with the operations used for sPDEs.

Our aim in this paper is to understand and formalize the grafting operations used for sPDEs. We now fix vector spaces
D_{E}
of decorations of the edges, and
D_{V}
of decorations of the vertices
D_{V}
,
and consider the space
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
of rooted trees where vertices are decorated by elements of
D_{V}
and edges by elements of
D_{E}
; we take in account the vector space structure of
D_{E}
and
D_{V}

by considering that the decorations are understood as linear in each vertex and each edge, which will lead us to define a tensor vector space of decorations for each tree, see Definition
1.1
.
We fix a linear map
\phi:D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
, which will represent the modification of the decorations, of the vertex on which one grafts, and of the edge created when one grafts.
We define for any
a\in D_{E}
a grafting product
\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}
on
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
. Here is an example of grafting in
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
: if
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5}\in D_{V}
and
a,a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in D_{E}
,

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\right),

where we used Sweedler’s like notations for
\phi
: if
a\in D_{E}
and
b\in D_{V}
, we put

\phi(a\otimes b)=\sum_{i}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b).

For an generic
\phi
, these products have no specific relations. However, we prove that they form a multiple pre-Lie algebra if, and only if, for any
a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E}
and any
b\in D_{V}
,

\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi_{E}^{j}(a^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}\circ\phi_{V}^{j}(b)=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi_{E}^{j}(a^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{V}^{j}\circ\phi_{V}^{i}(b).

This condition will be called the tree-compatibility of
\phi
and will be shortly denoted by the commutation condition

\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}=\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}:D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\otimes D_{V},

see Definition
1.2
. If
\phi
is tree-compatible, then it is possible to define an linear endomorphism
\Theta_{\phi}
of
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
,
acting on each decorated tree by the action of
\phi
on any pair
(e,s(e))
, where
e
is an edge and
s(e)
is the source of
e
; the tree-compatibility insures that this does not depend on the order chosen on the edges to perform the action of
\phi
. For example,

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.38889pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.26865pt\raise-15.6111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 35.77777pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 70.16666pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.04642pt\raise-15.6111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 31.38889pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.55554pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.38889pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.045pt\raise-15.90065pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{1})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 56.18059pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 110.97229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.2256pt\raise-15.90065pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{j}(a_{2})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 31.38889pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{i}\circ\phi_{V}^{j}(b_{3})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 115.36118pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

W prove that
\Theta_{\phi}
is a multiple pre-Lie algebra morphism from the free multiple pre-Lie algebra
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
to its
\phi
-deformed version
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
.
As a consequence, if
\phi
is tree-compatible and bijective, then
\phi^{-1}
is also tree-compatible and
\Theta_{\phi}
is an isomorphism, of inverse
\Theta_{\phi^{-1}}
, see Corollary
3.5
.
We also prove that
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
is generated by the space of trees with only one vertex if, and only if,
\phi
is surjective (Corollary
3.6
), and freely generated by these elements
if, and only if,
\phi
is bijective (Corollary
3.7
).
The pre-Lie algebra on
D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
can be seen as a pre-Lie algebra on planted decorated trees, which are obtained from decorated tree by adding a non-decorated root and a decorated edge. The Guin-Oudom construction
[
30
,
31
]
can be used on this pre-Lie algebra of planted rooted trees, which we do in Proposition
3.11
.
We obtain a Grossman-Larson-like Hopf algebra
[
21
,
22
]
.
For example, if
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}\in D_{E}
and
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4}\in D_{V}
,

The dual construction gives a Butcher-Connes-Kreimer Hopf algebra of planted rooted trees
[
10
,
32
,
25
]
. For example, if
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in D_{E}
and
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}\in D_{V}
,

Let us precise what we mean by "dual construction". When
D_{E}
and
D_{V}
are finite-dimensional, then we can identify the dual
(D_{E}\otimes D_{V})^{*}
with
D_{E}^{*}\otimes D_{V}^{*}
. Then, the transpose of an endomorphism
\phi
is tree-compatible if, and only if,
\phi
is tree-compatible.
More generally, we consider a pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
between
D_{E}^{\prime}\otimes D_{V}^{\prime}
and
D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
, maybe degenerate, and two tree-compatible maps
\phi
on
D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
and
D^{\prime}_{E}\otimes D^{\prime}_{V}
such that

\displaystyle\forall a\in D_{E},\>b\in D_{V},\>a^{\prime}\in D^{\prime}_{E},\>b^{\prime}\in D^{\prime}_{V},

\displaystyle\langle\phi^{\prime}(a^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime}),a\otimes b\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle a^{\prime}\otimes b(,\phi(a\otimes b)\rangle.

Then this pairing is extended into a Hopf pairing between the bialgebra associated by the Guin-Oudom construction to the pre-Lie algebra
(D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D^{\prime}_{E},D^{\prime}_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi^{\prime}})
and the Butcher-Connes-Kreimer bialgebra on
S(D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}))
associated to
\phi

(Theorem
3.20
).

We introduce several operations on tree-compatible maps: for example, we prove that if
\phi
is tree-compatible, then any polynomial in
\phi
is also tree-compatible and, under a condition of local nilpotency of
\phi
,
any formal series in
\phi
is also tree-compatible, see Proposition
2.4
.
This observation allows a better understanding of the structure used for sPDEs, firstly in the case without noise: in this case,
D_{E}^{\mathrm{s}}=D_{V}^{\mathrm{s}}=\mathrm{Vect}\left(\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\right)
. For any
i\in\llbracket 0;d\rrbracket
, a rather simple tree-compatible map is given by

\partial_{i}(a\otimes b)=\begin{cases}b_{i}\left(a-\epsilon^{(i)}\right)\otimes\left(b-\epsilon^{(i)}\right)\mbox{ if }a_{i},b_{i}\geq 1,\\
0\mbox{ otherwise},\end{cases}

where
(\epsilon^{(0)},\ldots,\epsilon^{(d)})
is the canonical basis of
\mathbb{N}^{d+1}
. We prove that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{K}^{d+1}
,
\partial^{\lambda}=\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{d}\lambda_{i}\partial_{i}
is tree-compatible and locally nilpotent,
so any formal series in
\partial^{\lambda}
is also tree-compatible: in particular, the map used for sPDEs is
\phi^{\mathrm{s}}=\exp(\partial_{0}+\cdots+\partial_{d})
, which is consequently tree-compatible:
we recover the fact that the products associated to this map are multiple pre-Lie
[
6
,
7
]
, and that
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E}^{\mathrm{s}},D_{V}^{\mathrm{s}}),\triangleleft)
is isomorphic to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E}^{\mathrm{s}},D_{V}^{\mathrm{s}}),\triangleleft^{\phi^{\mathrm{s}}})
,
through the explicit isomorphism
\Theta_{\phi^{\mathrm{s}}}
, whose inverse is the map
\Theta_{\phi^{-\mathrm{s}}}
associated to the tree-compatible map
\phi^{-\mathrm{s}}=\exp(-\partial_{0}-\cdots-\partial_{d})
,, see Theorem
4.6
.
For sPDEs with noise, one uses two other symbols
\Xi
on the edges and
\star
on the vertices. In our context, this is obtained by an operation of direct sum of tree-compatible maps (defined in Proposition
2.1
),
applied on
\phi^{\mathrm{s}}
and to the zero map of
\mathrm{Vect}(\Xi)\otimes\mathrm{Vect}(\star)
. We prove in Proposition
4.9
that the planted trees needed for sPDES are precisely the one generated by certain planted trees with only one edge (Proposition
4.10
).

Another important aspect of
[
6
]
is the definition of post-Lie algebraic structures. Roughly speaking, post-Lie algebras are Lie algebras with an extra product
\triangleleft
,
such that the sum of the Lie bracket and the antisymmetrization of
\triangleleft
is also a Lie bracket, see Definition
3.8
for details. In particular, pre-Lie algebras are post-Lie algebras whose underlying Lie bracket is abelian.
Firstly defined in an operadic context
[
33
]
, they turned out to describe the structure of a flat, constant torsion connection given by the Maurer–Cartan form on a Lie group,
see for example
[
1
,
2
,
6
,
11
,
13
,
14
,
17
,
29
,
28
]
for examples of applications in this setting.
The post-Lie structure of
[
6
]
is an extension of the pre-Lie structure on planted trees by the addition of generators
X_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq d
, acting on the decoration of the edge attached to the root for the bracket, and on the decoration of the vertices for
\triangleleft
.
We formalize this here with the help of a fixed post-Lie algebra
P
and two linear maps
\psi_{E}
and
\psi_{V}
, the first giving the action of
P
on the decorations of the edges and the second one the action of
P
on the vertices,
and we extend both the post-Lie structure on
P
and the pre-Lie structure on
D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
to
(D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}))\oplus P
using these actions.
We give the conditions that the triple
(\phi,\psi_{V},\psi_{E})
in Proposition
3.9
to obtain a post-Lie algebra. When
P
is a trivial post-Lie algebra, these conditions simplify, as shown in Corollary
3.10
.
We shown that these conditions are satisfied in the case coming from sPDEs, with or without noise, with
P=\mathrm{Vect}(x_{i}\mid 0\leq i\leq d)
as a trivial post-Lie algebra, and
\psi_{V}
and
\psi_{E}
defined by

\displaystyle\forall a,b\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1},

\displaystyle\psi_{E}(X_{i})(a)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a-\epsilon^{(i)}\mbox{ if }a_{i}\geq 1,\\
0\mbox{ otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\psi_{V}(X_{i})(b)

\displaystyle=b+\epsilon^{(i)},

\displaystyle\overline{\psi}_{E}(X_{i})(\Xi)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\overline{\psi}_{V}(X_{i})(\star)

\displaystyle=0.

see Propositions
4.8
and
4.9
. Here are examples of computations in the case of sPDEs: if a,
b_{1},b_{2},b_{4},b_{4},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}
,

3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

This text is organized as follows. In the first section, we precise the space of decorations associated to any rooted tree, and define tree-compatibility.
We study operations of tree-compatible maps (direct sums, polynomials, formal series, tensor products) in the second section.
We give the main theorem (Theorem
3.3
) on the pre-Lie multiple algebra associated to a tree-compatible map in the next section,
as well the construction of the morphism
\Theta_{\phi}
(Corollary
3.5
) and its applications.
This third section also contains results on the extension by a post-Lie algebra (Proposition
3.9
and Corollary
3.10
).
The last section contains examples, sPDEs with or without noises, giving back the structure of
[
6
]
, and a description of all tree-compatible maps in dimension
m\times 2
when one works over
\mathbb{C}
in Theorem
4.14
.
In the appendix, we prove that the pre-Lie algebra
D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E}\otimes D_{V})
, with the product
\triangleleft^{\phi}
associated to a tree-compatible map
\phi
, is free whenever
\phi
is invertible.

Notations 0.1
.

1.

We denote by
\mathbb{K}
a commutative field of characteristic zero. All the vector spaces of this text will be taken over this field.

2.

In the whole text,
D_{E}
and
D_{V}
are two nonzero vector spaces, which correspond to the set of decorations of the edges and of the vertices of the rooted trees will shall consider.

1
Construction

1.1
Vector space associated to a rooted tree

2.

Let
T\in\mathcal{T}
. The sets of vertices and edges of
T
are respectively denoted by
V(T)
and
E(T)
. The edges of
T
are oriented upwards, from the root to the leaves.
This defines two maps, the source
s
and the target
t
maps, both from
E(T)
to
V(T)
.

Remark 1.1
.

For any rooted tree
T
, the target map is injective, and its image is the set of vertices of
T
different from the root. The source map is generally not injective, except if
T
is a ladder:

\hbox to5.69pt{\vbox to4.27pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84523pt\lower-1.42271pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}

{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.38 0 C 1.38 0.76 0.76 1.38 0 1.38 C -0.76 1.38 -1.38 0.76 -1.38 0 C -1.38 -0.76 -0.76 -1.38 0 -1.38 C 0.76 -1.38 1.38 -0.76 1.38 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},\hbox to5.69pt{\vbox to11.38pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84523pt\lower-1.42271pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

Definition 1.1
.

1.

Let
T
be a rooted tree. The vector space associated to
T
is

D(T)=\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)}D_{E}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)}D_{V}.

The copy of
D_{E}
indexed by
e\in E(T)
will be denoted by
D_{e}
, and the copy of
D_{V}
indexed by
v\in V(T)
by
D_{v}
. Elements of
D(T)
are linear spans of tensors of the form

\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)}a_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)}b_{v},

where, for any
e\in E(T)
,
a_{e}\in D_{e}
is considered as the decoration of the edge
e
and for any
v\in V(T)
,
b_{v}\in D_{v}
is considered as the decoration of the vertex
v
.
In the sequel, we shall allow to permute the order of the factors in the tensor product of decorations of a given rooted tree, in order to simplify the writing if necessary.

2.

We denote by
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
the vector space

\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})=\bigoplus_{T\in\mathcal{T}}D(T).

Elements of this space are linear span of elements of the form

\displaystyle x=T\otimes\underbrace{\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)}a_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)}b_{v}}_{W_{T}},

(1)

where
T
is a rooted tree, written here to distinguish the different terms in the direct sum defining the space
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
.
In the sequel, each we shall use an element
x
,
x^{\prime}
or
x^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
, it will have the form of (
1
) with coherent
′
or
′′
everywhere it will be needed.

Here is a graphical representation of an element of
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
with four vertices and three edges, where
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in D_{E}
and
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4}\in D_{V}
:

15.26865pt\raise-46.8333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 35.77777pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 70.16666pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.04642pt\raise-46.8333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-62.4444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 31.38889pt\raise-62.4444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.55554pt\raise-62.4444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

We ordered the edges and vertices of
according to the depth-first search order to write the decorations of this tree as a tensor.

1.2
Actions on decorations

Definition 1.2
.

Let
\phi:D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
be a linear map.

•

We define two endomorphisms
\phi_{13}
and
\phi_{23}
of
D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
by

\displaystyle\phi_{23}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}}\otimes\phi,

\displaystyle\phi_{13}

\displaystyle=(\tau\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{D_{V}})\circ(\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}}\otimes\phi)\circ(\tau\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{D_{V}}),

where
\tau:D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{E}
is the usual flip.

•

We shall say that
\phi
is tree-compatible if
\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}=\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}
.

Notations 1.2
.

We adopt a Sweedler’s like notation for
\phi
: if
a\in D_{E}
,
b\in D_{V}
,

\phi(a\otimes b)=\sum_{i}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b).

If
a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E}
and
b\in D_{V}
,

\displaystyle\phi_{13}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b),

\displaystyle\phi_{23}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}a\otimes\phi_{E}^{i}(a^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b).

The tree-compatibility can be written as

\displaystyle\forall a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E},\>\forall b\in D_{V},

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi_{E}^{j}(a^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}\circ\phi_{V}^{j}(b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi_{E}^{j}(a^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{V}^{j}\circ\phi_{V}^{i}(b).

Notations 1.3
.

Let
T
be a rooted tree. For any
e_{0}\in E(T)
and
v_{0}\in V(T)
, we consider the endomorphism
\phi_{e_{0},v_{0}}
of
D(T)
defined by

\phi_{e_{0},v_{0}}=\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)\setminus\{e_{0}\}}\mathrm{Id}_{D_{e}}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}\mathrm{Id}_{D_{v}}\otimes\phi.

In other words,
\phi_{e_{0},v_{0}}
is given by the action of
\phi
on
D_{e_{0}}\otimes D_{v_{0}}
, the other decorations being unchanged. With Sweedler’s notation,

\phi_{e_{0},v_{0}}\left(\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)}a_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)}b_{v}\right)=\sum_{i}\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{e_{0}})\otimes\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)\setminus\{e_{0}\}}d_{e}\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b_{v_{0}})\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)\setminus\{v_{0}\}}b_{v}.

Lemma 1.3
.

Let us assume that
\phi
is tree-compatible. Let
T
be a rooted tree. For any
e,e^{\prime}\in E(T)
,
\phi_{e,s(e)}\circ\phi_{e^{\prime},s(e^{\prime})}=\phi_{e^{\prime},s(e^{\prime})}\circ\phi_{e,s(e)}
.

Proof.

If
s(e)\neq s(e^{\prime})
, this comes from the relation
\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{24}=\phi_{24}\circ\phi_{13}
as endomorphisms of
D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\otimes D_{V}
, with immediate notations.
If
e\neq e^{\prime}
and
s(e)=s(e^{\prime})
, this comes from the relation
\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}=\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}
.
∎

Definition 1.4
.

Let us assume that
\phi
is tree-compatible.

1.

We define, for any rooted tree
T
, putting
E(T)=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{k}\}
,

\phi_{T}=\phi_{e_{1},s(e_{1})}\circ\ldots\circ\phi_{e_{k},s(e_{k})}:D(T)\longrightarrow D(T).

By Lemma
1.3
, this does not depend on the choice of the order on the edges of
T
.

2.

We consider

\Theta_{\phi}=\bigoplus_{T\in\mathcal{T}}\phi_{T}:\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}).

Example 1.1
.

Let
a_{1},a_{2}\in D_{E}
and
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}\in D_{V}
.

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.38889pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.12024pt\raise-15.6111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{1}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.7292pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.54529pt\raise-15.6111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{1})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-17.7292pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{i}(b_{1})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array},

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.38889pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.26865pt\raise-15.6111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 35.77777pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 70.16666pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.04642pt\raise-15.6111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 31.38889pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.55554pt\raise-31.2222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.38889pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.045pt\raise-15.90065pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{1})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 56.18059pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 110.97229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.2256pt\raise-15.90065pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{j}(a_{2})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 31.38889pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{i}\circ\phi_{V}^{j}(b_{1})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 115.36118pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array},

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.38889pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.12024pt\raise-15.90065pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{1}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.12024pt\raise-45.8964pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{2}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise-59.9915pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.7292pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.38889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.54529pt\raise-15.90065pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{1})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-17.7292pt\raise-31.8013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{i}(b_{2})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.54529pt\raise-45.8964pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{j}(a_{2})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-17.7292pt\raise-59.9915pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{j}(b_{1})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

2
Operations on tree-compatible maps

2.1
Direct sums

Proposition 2.1
.

Let
\phi^{1}:D_{E}^{1}\otimes D_{V}^{1}\longrightarrow D_{E}^{1}\otimes D_{V}^{1}
and
\phi^{2}:D_{E}^{1}\otimes D_{V}^{2}\longrightarrow D_{E}^{2}\otimes D_{V}^{2}
be two maps, and let
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{K}
.
We define an endomorphism
\Phi=\phi^{1}\oplus_{\lambda,\mu}\phi^{2}
of
(D_{E}^{1}\oplus D_{E}^{2})\otimes(D_{V}^{1}\oplus D_{V}^{2})
by the following: for any
a_{i}\in D_{E}^{i}
,
b_{i}\in D_{E}^{i}
, with
i=1
or
2
,

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1}\otimes b_{1})

\displaystyle=\phi^{1}(a_{1}\otimes b_{1}),

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{2}\otimes b_{2})

\displaystyle=\phi^{2}(a_{2}\otimes b_{2}),

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1}\otimes b_{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda a_{1}\otimes b_{2},

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{2}\otimes b_{1})

\displaystyle=\mu a_{2}\otimes b_{1}.

Then
\Phi
is tree-compatible if, and only if,
\phi^{1}
and
\phi^{2}
are tree-compatible.

Proof.

\Longrightarrow
. Note that
\Phi_{\mid D_{E}^{i}\otimes D_{V}^{i}}=\phi^{i}
. Therefore, by restriction, if
\Phi
is tree-compatible, then
\phi^{i}
is compatible for
i=1
or
i=2
.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
a_{i},a^{\prime}_{i}\in D_{E}^{i}
and
b_{i}\in D_{V}^{i}
for
i\in\llbracket 1;2\rrbracket
. Let us show that

\Phi_{23}\circ\Phi_{13}(a_{i}\otimes a^{\prime}_{j}\otimes b_{k})=\Phi_{13}\circ\Phi_{23}(a_{i}\otimes a^{\prime}_{j}\otimes b_{k})

for
(i,j,k)\in\{1,2\}^{3}
.

•

If
(i,j,k)=(1,1,1)
or
(2,2,2)
, this comes from the tree-compatibility of
\phi^{1}
and
\phi^{2}
.

•

If
(i,j,k)=(1,2,1)
, as
\Phi_{13}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{2}\otimes b_{1})\in D_{E}^{1}\otimes D_{E}^{2}\otimes D_{E}^{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{13}\circ\Phi_{23}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{2}\otimes b_{1})

\displaystyle=\mu\Phi_{13}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{2}\otimes b_{1}),

\displaystyle\Phi_{23}\circ\Phi_{13}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{2}\otimes b_{1})

\displaystyle=\mu\Phi_{13}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{2}\otimes b_{1}).

The computation is similar for
(i,j,k)=(2,1,1)
,
(2,1,2)
, and
(1,2,2)
.

•

If
(i,j,k)=(1,1,2)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{13}\circ\Phi_{23}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda\mu a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2},

\displaystyle\Phi_{23}\circ\Phi_{13}(a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda\mu a_{1}\otimes a^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2}.

The computation is similar for
(i,j,k)=(2,2,1)
. ∎

2.2
Sums and compositions

Lemma 2.2
.

1.

\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}\otimes D_{V}}
is tree-compatible. Moreover,
\Theta_{\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}\otimes D_{V}}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})}
.

2.

Let
\phi:D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
be a bijection. If
\phi
is tree-compatible, then
\phi^{-1}
is tree-compatible. Moreover,
\Theta_{\phi}^{-1}=\Theta_{\phi^{-1}}
.

Proof.

Direct verifications.
∎

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
\phi,\psi:D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
be two tree-compatible maps, such that

\phi_{13}\circ\psi_{23}=\psi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}.

Then
\phi\circ\psi
is tree-compatible and for any
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{K}^{2}
,
\alpha\phi+\beta\psi
is tree-compatible.

Proof.

Let us first prove that
\psi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}=\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}
. We shall use the map

\tau:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\otimes D_{V}&\longrightarrow&D_{E}\otimes D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\\
a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes b&\longmapsto&a^{\prime}\otimes a\otimes b.\end{array}\right.

Then

\displaystyle\phi_{13}\circ\psi_{23}

\displaystyle=\tau\circ\phi_{23}\circ\tau\circ\tau\circ\psi_{13}\circ\tau

\displaystyle=\tau\circ\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}\circ\tau

\displaystyle=\tau\circ\psi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}\circ\tau

\displaystyle=\tau\circ\psi_{13}\circ\tau\circ\tau\circ\phi_{23}\circ\tau=\psi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(\phi\circ\psi)_{13}\circ(\phi\circ\psi)_{23}

\displaystyle=\phi_{13}\circ\psi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{23}

\displaystyle=\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}\circ\psi_{23}

\displaystyle=\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}\circ\psi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}

\displaystyle=\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}\circ\psi_{13}=(\phi\circ\psi)_{23}\circ(\phi\circ\psi)_{13}.

So
\phi\circ\psi
is tree-compatible.

If
\Phi=\alpha\phi+\beta\psi
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{13}\circ\Phi_{23}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}+\alpha\beta\phi_{13}\circ\psi_{23}+\alpha\beta\psi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}+\beta^{2}\psi_{13}\circ\psi_{23}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}+\alpha\beta\psi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}+\alpha\beta\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}+\beta^{2}\psi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}=\Phi_{23}\circ\Phi_{13}.\qed

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
\phi
be tree-compatible map.

1.

For any
P\in\mathbb{K}[X]
,
P(\phi)
is tree-compatible.

2.

Let us assume that
\phi
is locally nilpotent, that is to say

\displaystyle\forall x\in D_{E}\otimes D_{V},\>\exists n\geq 1,\>\phi^{n}(x)=0.

Then, for any
P\in\mathbb{K}[[X]]
,
P(\phi)
is tree-compatible.

Proof.

The tree-compatibility of
\phi
implies that for any
P,Q\in\mathbb{K}[X]
,

P(\phi)_{13}\circ Q(\phi)_{23}=P(\phi_{13})\circ Q(\phi_{23})=Q(\phi_{23})\circ P(\phi_{13})=Q(\phi)_{23}\circ P(\phi)_{13}.

In particular, for
P=Q
,
P(\phi)
is tree-compatible.

If
\phi
is locally nilpotent and
P\in\mathbb{K}[[X]]
, as
P(\phi)
is equal to a polynomial in
\phi
when applied to any element of
D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
, the first point implies the result for
P(\phi)
.
∎

2.3
Tensor product

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
\phi:D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
and
\phi^{\prime}:D^{\prime}_{E}\otimes D^{\prime}_{V}\longrightarrow D^{\prime}E\otimes D^{\prime}_{V}
be two tree-compatible maps.
Then
\Phi=\phi_{13}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{24}:(D_{E}\otimes D_{E}^{\prime})\otimes(D_{V}\otimes D_{V}^{\prime})\longrightarrow(D_{E}\otimes D_{E}^{\prime})\otimes(D_{V}\otimes D_{V}^{\prime})
is tree-compatible.
With Sweedler’s notation,

\Phi(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime})=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\phi_{E}^{i}(a)\otimes\phi^{\prime j}_{E}(a^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b)\otimes\phi^{\prime j}_{V}(b^{\prime}).

Proof.

In this case,

\displaystyle\Phi_{13}

\displaystyle=\phi_{15}\circ\psi_{26},

\displaystyle\Phi_{23}

\displaystyle=\phi_{35}\circ\psi_{46}.

Moreover,
\phi_{15}
,
\psi_{26}
,
\phi_{35}
and
\psi_{46}
pairwise commute, either as they do not act on the same factors of the tensor product
(D_{E}\otimes D_{E}^{\prime})^{\otimes 2}\otimes(D_{V}\otimes D_{V}^{\prime})
,
or because of the tree-compatibility of
\phi
and
\psi
(for
\phi_{15}
and
\phi_{35}
,
\psi_{26}
and
\psi_{46}
). So
\Phi_{13}
and
\Phi_{23}
commute.
∎

3
Pre- and post-Lie structures associated to a tree-compatible map

3.1
Multiple pre-Lie algebras

Definition 3.1
.

[
7
,
18
,
35
]

A
D_{E}
-multiple (or
D_{E}
-matching, or shortly, multiple) pre-Lie algebra is a pair
(V,\triangleleft)
where
V
is a vector space and

\triangleleft:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}D_{E}\otimes V\otimes V&\longrightarrow&V\\
a\otimes x\otimes y&\longmapsto&x\triangleleft_{a}y\end{array}\right.

is a linear map such that

\displaystyle\forall a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E},\>\forall x,y,z\in V,

\displaystyle x\triangleleft_{a}(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z)-(x\triangleleft_{a}y)\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z

\displaystyle=y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}(x\triangleleft_{a}z)-(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}x)\triangleleft_{a}z.

In particular, for any
a\in D_{E}
,
(V,\triangleleft_{a})
is a pre-Lie algebra.

Let us recall the following Lemma, proved in
[
19
, Proposition 2.4]
in a more general setting.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
(V,\triangleleft)
be a
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie algebra. Then
D_{E}\otimes V
is a pre-Lie algebra, with the product defined by

\displaystyle\forall a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E},\>\forall x,y\in V,

\displaystyle a\otimes x\triangleleft a^{\prime}\otimes y

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}\otimes x\triangleleft_{a}y.

Proof.

Let
a,a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime}\in D_{E}
and
x,y,z\in V
.

\displaystyle a\otimes x\triangleleft(a^{\prime}\otimes y\triangleleft a^{\prime\prime}\otimes z)-(a\otimes x\triangleleft a^{\prime}\otimes y)\triangleleft a^{\prime\prime}\otimes z

\displaystyle=a\otimes x\triangleleft(a^{\prime\prime}\otimes y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z)-(a^{\prime}\otimes x\triangleleft_{a}y)\triangleleft a^{\prime\prime}\otimes z

\displaystyle=a^{\prime\prime}\otimes\left(x\triangleleft_{a}(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z)-(x\triangleleft_{a}y)\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}z\right),

whereas

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\otimes y\triangleleft(a\otimes x\triangleleft a^{\prime\prime}\otimes z)-(a^{\prime}\otimes y\triangleleft a\otimes z)\triangleleft a^{\prime\prime}\otimes z

\displaystyle=a^{\prime\prime}\otimes\left(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}(x\triangleleft_{a}z)-(y\triangleleft_{a^{\prime}}x)\triangleleft_{a}z\right).

Therefore, the
D_{E}
-multiple relation for
V
implies (and in fact, is equivalent to) the pre-Lie relation for
D_{E}\otimes V
.
∎

Notations 3.1
.

Let
T,T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}
and
v\in V(T)
. We denote by
T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}
the rooted tree obtained by grafting
T
and the vertex
v
of
T^{\prime}
, creating a new edge
e
with
s(e)=v
and
t(e)
equal to the root of
T
.

From
[
18
, Corollary 2.7]
and
[
19
, Theorem 2.2]
for a more general setting, the free
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie algebra generated by
D_{V}
is, as a vector space,
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
, with the product
\triangleleft
defined by

\displaystyle\left(T\otimes\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)}a_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)}b_{v}\right)\triangleleft_{a}\left(T^{\prime}\otimes\bigotimes_{e\in E(T^{\prime})}a^{\prime}_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}b^{\prime}_{v}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V(T)}T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes a\otimes\bigotimes_{e\in E(T)}a_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{e\in E(T^{\prime})}a^{\prime}_{e}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T)}b_{v}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}b^{\prime}_{v},

where the factor
a
in this tensor is understood as the decoration of the edge created when
T
is grafted on the vertex
v
of
T^{\prime}
. In a more compact way,

T\otimes W_{T}\triangleleft_{a}T^{\prime}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}=\sum_{v\in V(T)}T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}.

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
\phi:D_{E}\otimes D_{V}\longrightarrow D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
be a map. We then define a map

\triangleleft^{\phi}:D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})

by

\displaystyle T\otimes W_{T}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}T^{\prime}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}=\sum_{v\in V(T)}\sum T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes\phi_{1,v}\left(a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\right).

Here,
\phi_{1,v}
means that
\phi
acts on
a\otimes d^{\prime}_{v}
.
Then
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
is a
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie algebra if, and only if,
\phi
is tree-compatible.

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.14583pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.14583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.47223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.03113pt\raise-15.78482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{i}(a)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 54.25002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 109.3542pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.75891pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b^{\prime}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.63011pt\raise-15.78482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{j}(a^{\prime})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.56966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 29.14583pt\raise-31.56966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{j}\circ\phi_{V}^{i}(b^{\prime\prime})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 112.9028pt\raise-31.56966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

We obtain from the multiple pre-Lie relation, identifying the decorations of the vertices and of the edges, that

\phi_{15}\circ\phi_{25}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime\prime})=\phi_{25}\circ\phi_{15}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime\prime}).

As
b,b^{\prime}\neq 0
, this gives
\phi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}=\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{13}
, so
\phi
is tree-compatible.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
x=T\otimes W_{T}
,
x^{\prime}=T^{\prime}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}
and
x^{\prime\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}}
be elements of
\mathcal{T}(V_{E},V_{T})
. Let
a,a^{\prime}\in D_{E}
.

\displaystyle x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}\left(x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}\right)-\left(x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime}\right)\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v^{\prime\prime}\in V(T^{\prime\prime})}x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\otimes\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}}(a^{\prime}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle-\sum_{v^{\prime}\in V(T^{\prime})}T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\otimes\phi_{1,v^{\prime}}(a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}})\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1},v^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in V(T^{\prime\prime})}T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}(T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}T^{\prime\prime})\otimes\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\circ\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v^{\prime\prime}\in V(T^{\prime\prime}),\\
v^{\prime}\in V(T^{\prime})\end{subarray}}T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime}}(T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}}T^{\prime\prime})\otimes\phi_{1,v^{\prime}}\circ\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v^{\prime\prime}\in V(T^{\prime\prime}),\\
v^{\prime}\in V(T^{\prime})\end{subarray}}(T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime}}T^{\prime})\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\otimes\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}}\circ\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}}).

Let
v^{\prime}\in V(T^{\prime})
and
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(T^{\prime\prime})
. Then the two following rooted trees are equal:

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime}}(T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=(T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime}}T^{\prime})\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}.

Moreover,
\phi_{1,v^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}}
commute, are they apply on different factors of the tensor product
D_{T_{1}}=D_{T_{2}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}\left(x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}\right)-\left(x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime}\right)\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1},v^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in V(T^{\prime\prime})}T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}(T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}T^{\prime\prime})\otimes\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\circ\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}\left(x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime\prime}\right)-\left(x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x\right)\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1},v^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in V(T^{\prime\prime})}T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}T^{\prime\prime})\otimes\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\circ\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}(a\otimes a^{\prime}\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\otimes W_{T^{\prime\prime}}).

Let
v^{\prime\prime}_{1},v^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in V(T^{\prime\prime})
. The rooted trees
T_{1}=T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}(T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}T^{\prime\prime})
and
T_{2}=T^{\prime}\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(T\curvearrowright_{v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}T^{\prime\prime})
are equal.
Moreover:

•

If
v^{\prime\prime}_{1}\neq v^{\prime\prime}_{2}
,
\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
and
\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}
commute, as they apply on different factors of the tensor product
D_{T_{1}}=D_{T_{2}}
.

•

If
v^{\prime\prime}_{1}=v^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, as
\phi
is tree-compatible,
\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
and
\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
commute.

As a conclusion, in any case,
\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\circ\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}=\phi_{2,v^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\circ\phi_{1,v^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
. We obtain

\displaystyle x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}\left(x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}\right)-\left(x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime}\right)\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}\left(x\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime\prime}\right)-\left(x^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a^{\prime}}x\right)\triangleleft^{\phi}_{a}x^{\prime\prime}.

So
(\mathcal{T}(V_{E},V_{D}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
is
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie.
∎

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
\phi,\psi
be two tree-compatible maps on
D_{E}\otimes D_{V}
such that

\psi_{13}\circ\phi_{23}=\phi_{23}\circ\psi_{13}.

Then
\Theta_{\phi}
is a
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie algebra map from
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\psi})
to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi\circ\psi})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.3
,
\phi\circ\psi
is tree-compatible. Let us prove that
\Theta_{\phi}
is a multiple pre-Lie morphism.
Let
x=T\otimes W_{T}
and
x^{\prime}=T^{\prime}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}
be two elements of
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
, and let
a\in D_{E}
.

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}(x\triangleleft^{\psi}_{a}x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}\Theta_{\phi}(T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes\psi_{1,v}(a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes\prod^{\circ}_{e\in E(T)}\phi_{e,s(e)}\circ\prod^{\circ}_{e^{\prime}\in E(T^{\prime})}\phi_{e^{\prime},s(e^{\prime})}\circ\phi_{1,v}\circ\psi_{1,v}(a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes\phi_{1,v}\circ\psi_{1,v}\circ\prod^{\circ}_{e\in E(T)}\phi_{e,s(e)}\circ\prod^{\circ}_{e^{\prime}\in E(T^{\prime})}\phi_{e^{\prime},s(e^{\prime})}(a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}T\curvearrowright_{v}T^{\prime}\otimes(\phi\circ\psi)_{1,v}\left(a\otimes\prod^{\circ}_{e\in E(T)}\phi_{e,s(e)}(W_{T})\otimes\prod^{\circ}_{e^{\prime}\in E(T^{\prime})}\phi_{e^{\prime},s(e^{\prime})}(W_{T^{\prime}})\right)

\displaystyle=\Theta_{\phi}(x)\triangleleft^{\phi\circ\psi}_{a}\Theta_{\phi}(x^{\prime}).

The symbols
\displaystyle\prod^{\circ}
have to be understood as compositions, without any constraint on the order by the tree-compatibility of
\phi
.
The commutation on the third equality comes from the fact that
\phi
is tree-compatible (for the commutation of
\phi_{1,v}
) and from the hypothesis on
\phi
and
\psi
(for the commutation of
\psi_{1,v}
).
So
\Theta_{\phi}
is a
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie algebra morphism from
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\psi})
to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi\circ\psi})
.
∎

In the particular case where
\psi=\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}\otimes D_{V}}
, which is tree-compatible by Lemma
2.2
, note that
\triangleleft^{\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}\otimes D_{V}}}=\triangleleft
. We obtain:

Corollary 3.5
.

1.

The map
\Theta_{\phi}
is a multiple pre-Lie algebra morphism from
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
.

2.

If
\phi
is a bijective tree-compatible map, then
\Theta_{\phi}:(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
is an isomorphism, of inverse
\Theta_{\phi^{-1}}
.

Corollary 3.6
.

Let
\phi
be tree-compatible. Then
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
is generated by the trees

in

.

\Longrightarrow
. Let

under the form

3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{i}(b_{k})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}.

This gives

a\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i}\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{k})\otimes\phi_{V}^{i}(b_{k})\otimes b^{\prime}_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\phi\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{D_{V}})(a_{k}\otimes b_{k}\otimes b^{\prime}_{k}).

Let
\lambda\in D_{V}^{*}
, such that
\lambda(b^{\prime})=1
. Applying
\mathrm{Id}_{D_{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{D_{V}}\otimes\lambda
, we obtain

a\otimes b=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda(b^{\prime}_{k})\phi(a_{k}\otimes b_{k}),

so
a\otimes b\in\mathrm{Im}(\phi)
:
\phi
is surjective.

\Longleftarrow
. Then
\Theta_{\phi}
is surjective. As it is a surjective
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie morphism from
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
sending
\textstyle{b}
to itself for any
b\in D_{V}
,
and as
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
is generated by these elements, so is
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
.
∎

Corollary 3.7
.

Let
\phi
be tree-compatible. Then
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
is freely generated by the trees
\hbox to5.69pt{\vbox to4.27pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84523pt\lower-1.42271pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@discardpath@clipped{M -3.94 -1.97 M -3.94 -1.97 L -3.94 3.94 L 3.94 3.94 L 3.94 -1.97 Z M 3.94 3.94} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptsize
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.38 0 C 1.38 0.76 0.76 1.38 0 1.38 C -0.76 1.38 -1.38 0.76 -1.38 0 C -1.38 -0.76 -0.76 -1.38 0 -1.38 C 0.76 -1.38 1.38 -0.76 1.38 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\otimes b=\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.14583pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.14583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.47223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
, with
b\in D_{V}
, if and only if,
\phi
is bijective.

Proof.

\Longrightarrow
. By Corollary
3.6
,
\phi
is surjective.
The unique
D_{E}
-multiple pre-Lie morphism from
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
sending
\textstyle{b}
to itself for any
b\in D_{V}
, is bijective.
This morphism is obviously
\Theta_{\phi}
. Let
\displaystyle x=\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{k}\otimes b_{k}\in\mathrm{Ker}(\phi)
. Then

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.60591pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-6.5486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.75891pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b^{\prime}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.3546pt\raise-15.78482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{k}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.60591pt\raise-31.56966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{k}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.94621pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-6.5486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.75891pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b^{\prime}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.77965pt\raise-15.78482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30008pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{E}^{i}(a_{k})}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-17.94621pt\raise-31.56966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.88043pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\phi_{V}^{i}(b_{k})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}=0.

As
\Theta_{\phi}
is an isomorphism, we deduce that

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.60591pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-6.5486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.75891pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b^{\prime}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.3546pt\raise-15.78482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{k}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-7.60591pt\raise-31.56966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{k}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}=0.

As
b^{\prime}\neq 0
, this gives
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{k}\otimes b_{k}=0
, so
\phi
is injective.

\Longleftarrow
. Then
\Theta_{\phi}
is an isomorphism from
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
to
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
sending
\textstyle{b}
to itself for any
b\in D_{V}
,
and as
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft)
is freely generated by these elements, so is
(\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V}),\triangleleft^{\phi})
.
∎

3.2
Associated pre- and post-Lie algebras

Let
\phi
be tree-compatible. By Lemma
3.2
and Theorem
3.3
,
D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
is a pre-Lie algebra.
Elements
a\otimes T\otimes W_{T}\in D_{E}\otimes\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
, where
T
is a decorated rooted tree, are identified with planted tree by adding a non decorated root to
T
, the created edge being decorated by
a
, as in
[
6
]
.
For example, if
a_{1},a_{2},a\in D_{E}
and

, we shall identify the two elements

and

Let us describe this pre-Lie structure. Let

, and

,

two elements of
\mathcal{T}(D_{E},D_{V})
. Then

\displaystyle a\otimes x\triangleleft^{\phi}a^{\prime}\otimes x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v_{0}\in V(T^{\prime})}a^{\prime}\otimes T\curvearrowright_{v_{0}}T^{\prime}\otimes\phi_{1,v_{0}}\left(a\otimes W_{T}\otimes W_{T^{\prime}}\right).

.
